This paper has a real idea: it isolates the post-training threshold-calibration channel in federated IoT anomaly detection, keeps training/model/test artifacts clean, and shows policy-dependent threshold displacement under a tightly scoped score-level contamination model on N-BaIoT with FedAvg autoencoders.  The strongest part is its **scope discipline** relative to many security papers: it repeatedly says the attack is score-level, post-training, single-client, and not an end-to-end traffic-generation result.  The biggest problems are that the evidence base is still narrow, the statistical gate system is custom and under-justified, Cluster policy is under-specified, and there is at least one serious internal inconsistency about spillover that a careful reviewer will notice immediately. **Main reason to accept:** the paper identifies a plausible and under-discussed attack surface that standard training/aggregation defenses do not directly monitor, and it studies it in a controlled, policy-comparative way with explicit sanity checks that preserve weights, labels, and AUROC. **Main reason to reject:** the paper’s strongest claims rest on one dataset, one detector family, one FL algorithm, one quantile, one compromise pattern, and a score-level proxy whose deployment realism is explicitly left for future work, while some methodological choices are not justified enough to carry top-tier confidence. ## 4. Top Weaknesses

1. **Severity: fatal / near-fatal.** Contributions item 3 says Local and Cluster have “no cross-client spillover,” but Section 4.2 and Section 5.4 explicitly say Cluster can cause intra-cluster spillover to co-cluster members. This is a direct contradiction that undermines trust in the paper’s central policy-differentiation story. **Location:** p.2 Contributions item 3 vs. p.5 Section 4.2 vs. p.8 Section 5.4. 5. **Severity: major.** Spillover is asserted more strongly than it is measured. Global spillover is definitional because all clients share a threshold, but the paper mostly reports victim \(\Delta\)TPR and does not directly tabulate harmed non-victim clients or spillover magnitude by client. **Location:** p.4 Threat model; p.7–8 Sections 5.2 and 5.4; Table 2 p.11. ## 5. Fatal or Near-Fatal Risks

- **Internal contradiction on Cluster spillover.** If not fixed, this alone can trigger a trust collapse because it affects the paper’s main contribution and appears in high-visibility text. **Location:** p.2, p.5, p.8. ## 11. Visual and Formatting Review

The paper looks structurally professional enough for submission, but several presentation choices are leaving reviewer goodwill on the table.  The biggest problem is not cosmetic polish; it is that some of the most important methodological definitions are compressed into dense inline prose or formulas instead of being displayed and made easy to audit.
